182. **An image is filtered once** [Core `DE_CStandardImage::AssureUpdatedImage` 0x701950f0, `ObjProcessContents` 0x70194420, `ReadingComplete` 0x70196e30, `DE_CImage::NotifyFilterInvalid` 0x7018cc80; OMF `DE_COMF2FighterImageFilter::Filter` 0x350350e0, `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManagerRobot::UpdateModel` 0x35055a10 / `ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x35055890, `DE_CFighterModelDescription::NewStrippedFighterModel` 0x35038940]: the image decodes (and runs its filters) only while +0x9c is 1, which reading its contents sets, and decoding sets 4; nothing else sets it back (`NotifyFilterInvalid` is empty), so a colour change after the first decode is never shown. The model picker repaints by deleting its fighter and model and making new instances (`UpdateModel`, on its buttons and when the robot is made) with the last selected pilot. `Filter` adds a scaled image only when its width (+0x128) and height (+0x12c) equal the filtered image's, else it reports an error: Mantis's 256² bug images and the model's 1024² `RGBScaled` never match, so the bugs get no scaled pass.
183. **When a state is recorded, and in what time** [Core `DE_CStateManager::AddCurrentState` 0x70126250, `AddToNewStateList` / `AddToStateList` 0x701b1b40 / 0x701b19f0, `CleanOldStates` 0x701b1c90, `ProcessEvent` 0x701b18c0, `ObjProcessContents` 0x701b1600, `ObjInitContents` 0x701b1310, `DE_CStandardStateManager::CleanState` 0x701af300, `DE_CStateManagerRecord::Record` 0x70203fa0, `DE_CSessionRecord::Close` 0x70203810, `DE_CStateManager::JoinStateManagerRecord` / `UnjoinStateManagerRecord` 0x701b0dc0 / 0x701b0e90; Creature `RegisterExecutorState` 0x15015650, `LocalTime` 0x15008300, `ObjPrepare` 0x15016050, `RepackState` 0x15015380; OMF `DE_CFighterPhysics::PackState` 0x3503a5d0 (listing) / `RepackState` 0x3503aca0, `DE_COMF2Fighter::PackState` 0x3502a4e0, `RegisterGetHitState` 0x350296b0]: a state's time is its owner's local time: a tick-based creature's +0x1d4, its tick count since its creation time (+0x1d8; ObjPrepare sets +0x1d4 = (now − creation) / rate), so a fighter's states are stamped in ticks, not ms; anything else the scene's ms. Each add to the history counts twice in +0x64 (new-state list and state list); past 40 `CleanOldStates(0)` records every state older than the window behind the newest, keeping at most 22, the window being 1000 / +0xb8 owner units (+0xb8 is 1.0, or a tick-based creature's rate set by its ObjPrepare: 62 ticks for a fighter). A state at the same time as one in the history replaces it unrecorded. The owner's pre-destruction event records all of its manager's states; `Close` flushes only each record's first joined manager. `Record` drops a last recorded state of the same time and repacks through the record's first manager's owner (with that owner dead: "Object Dead", nothing recorded): executor statuses byte for byte with each reference resolved now (a dead object becomes null), and a fighter's physics unpacked into its scratch physics (+0x1ac) and packed again, so its mask is recomputed from the unpacked values (a mode-2 guide always sends a null hit-by; a mode-1 guide whose last hit died is dropped) and bit 0x02 is set when the live fighter's +0x4a0 is, i.e. when `RegisterGetHitState` ran since its last `PackState`. A manager joins the session of its scene (`DE_CScene` +0xa8) only with one of its contents BOOLs +0x84/+0x88/+0x8c set (defaults 0, 1, 1), and the record only with +0x8c, so a sub-session's record holds only its own objects; owners with the same ID (two sessions' slot pickers, empty IDs) share a record.
184. **Restoring a replayed creature: its clock** [Creature `DE_CCreature::UnpackState` 0x15015160, `SetTime` 0x15015d20, `RewindTime` 0x15015eb0, `Tick` 0x15016870, `AssureSequenced` 0x15015be0, `ObjProcessContents` 0x15014820 (+0x1e0, the second u32 of contents v2-v4); Core `DE_CStandardStateManager::UpdateForReplay` 0x701af350, `AddLaggedState` 0x701aec10, `AddLaggedStateDestIndex` 0x701ae2b0, `DE_CScene::TemporalUpdate` 0x70070710; OMF `DE_COMF2Fighter::Tick` 0x350253a0]: `UpdateForReplay` looks a record up by the owner's local time (a fighter's ticks). `AddLaggedState` puts it after the newest held state at or before its time and discards the newer ones, except that the same data at the same time is kept and not restored again (the newer ones stay too). `UnpackState` sets +0x1d4 to the state's time, runs instant instances with the scene clock at that tick, unpacks the physics and calls `SetTime(now)` at once, or with +0x1e0 set (the fighters) from the scene's +0x98 list at the next `TemporalUpdate`. `SetTime` re-ticks a creature behind the clock (scene time pinned to each tick, at most 100 ticks, +0x1d4 jumping to target − 100 first), sets +0x1d4 and +0x1e4 to the target, and calls `RewindTime` for one ahead of it. `Tick` with +0x2fc 0 restores a held state of exactly its tick (other than the one being unpacked, +0x214) and then skips physics and brain; `DE_COMF2Fighter::Tick` skips guide and trailers while +0x2fc is 2.
185. **A replay's hold after going back, and the 2000 ms jump** [Core `DE_CScene::PassTime` 0x70070040, `DE_CTemporalMasterScene::TemporalGlobalTime` 0x70078910 / `TemporalPassTime` 0x70078bb0; Creature `RewindTime` 0x15015eb0, `Tick` 0x15016870, `AssureSequenced` 0x15015be0; OMF `DE_CVCRControls::ObjUpdate` 0x35064ff0]: when the clock moves back, or forward by more than 2000 ms in a replaying session (+0xa8 → +0xa0), `PassTime` moves +0x160 by the delta (back) or by the excess (forward, then simulates 2000), restarts each temporal set at its last tick before the new time and calls each element's `SetTime`. A tick-based creature going back holds (+0x2fc = 2, +0x1e8 = its tick then, +0x1e4 = its tick before this rewind, or a pending fix-up's) unless the rewind lands exactly there; while held it ticks (+0x1d4 counting, the command manager running) without physics or brain. The hold ends at the end of the first tick whose +0x1d4 has reached +0x1e4, which a restored state's `SetTime` sets to the current tick; `AssureSequenced` then, short of +0x1e8, re-takes the record (`UpdateForReplay`), restores the newest held state at or before now and re-ticks from its time (unless the manager's +0xd0 BOOL), or past +0x1e8 re-ticks from it. Since a re-added identical state is not restored, a short rewind leaves replayed fighters frozen until the clock is back where it was. Creatures with no tick rate never hold. The VCR's −2000 / +2000 pair cancels in `TemporalPassTime`'s accumulator; the 2000 ms cap is `PassTime`'s.
186. **CData values, the scene's name, object info +0x84** [Core `DataGenerateContentsCaller` / `DataProcessContentsCaller` 0x7014b5f0 / 0x7014b620, `DE_CScene::ObjInitContents` 0x7006e8d0 / `ObjName` 0x700744a0 / `ObjParent` 0x700736c0, `DE_CActor::ObjSetParent` 0x7008afb0, `DE_CObject::ObjPath` 0x7003a880; `DE_CTransferObjectInfo::DE_CTransferObjectInfo` 0x70055370 / `GenerateHeader` 0x70055690 / `ProcessHeader` 0x700557c0; OMF `PackForGetHitEffect` / `UnpackForGetHitEffect` 0x35016e00 / 0x35016f30]: a replicated CData is its DataBlock as a squeezed length and the bytes; the hit effect's is squeezed 1, the damage byte, squeezed effect (+0x48), reaction (+0x44) and attack (+0x4c) types, the connect point and both effect directions (12 bytes each) and the flags byte. A session's scene is named "{No Name}" and has no parent (+0x38, set only by `ObjSetParent`), so a root actor's ObjPath starts "{No Name}\". Object info +0x84 is set only by its constructor (0) and `ProcessHeader` (v3+): no retail code assigns or reads it otherwise, so a new record's info writes 0 and a renamed one writes back what was loaded; the shipped files' values (small counts in most files, large numbers in the fighter files) come from the authoring tools.
187. **`Scene()` names the object's scene; `Scene().Add` from the GUI** [Core `DE_CScene::Add` 0x7006ed50 (+0x10 the actor's scene), `DE_CTopControl3d::AssureRenderObjects` 0x70103870; Session `DE_CStandardSession::Scene` 0x4500efa0; scripts `BActorSpawner`, `BCrowdResponseSlot`]: `Scene()` is a property of the object it is called on, not of the caller's context: an actor's is the scene it was added to (+0x10), a session's or modifier's its session's scene. A GUI actor's script therefore adds to the GUI's scene from any entry point (temporal, event handler, render tick). Ours keeps one `Scene` object for the game's scenes (sessions told apart by the caller) and gives each GUI scene (the top control's, a scene viewer's) its own, which `CScene::Add`, `Time`, `PreciseTime`, `NumActors`, `Actor`, `IsPaused` and `GatherAllActorsWithProperty` read.
188. **`IsPaused` is the scene's own pause** [Core `IsPausedCaller` 0x7006de90, `DE_CScene::ObjInitContents` 0x7006e8d0, `Genesis` 0x70070900, `Pause` 0x700729e0 / `Continue` 0x700729f0 (vtable +0x144 / +0x148), `TemporalUpdate` 0x70070710, `AssureTemporalMaster` 0x70072a00, `DE_CTemporalMasterScene::ObjInitContents` 0x700787a0 / `BeginSceneUpdate` 0x70078b30, `DE_CSession::Update` 0x701f8ab0; Session `DE_CStandardSession::Genesis`, `DE_CStandardSessionModifier::OwnEnvironment` 0x450161e0; OMF `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManagerRobot` (Pause after `Genesis`, `Continue` on its idle event); script `BRulesDisplay` (`Done.Update`)]: +0xf0 is 1 from `ObjInitContents` until `Genesis` (scene time started) clears it; `Pause` / `Continue` set and clear it, and the model picker's robot is their only retail caller. A session's scene gets `Genesis` when a modifier owns the environment (through the scene viewer's `EnsureSceneTimeStarted`); `Genesis` passes on to a temporal master made before it (`BSinglePlayerSubSession` makes it in `Entry`, before the environment), so `Scene().TemporalMaster().IsPaused()`, its only script use, is false once the environment is in. The top control's scene gets `Genesis` in `AssureRenderObjects`. A paused scene that is not itself a temporal master (+0xec) passes no time (`TemporalUpdate`), and `DE_CSession::Update` treats a paused scene like autopause. Ours had returned autopause, which happens not to stop `BRulesDisplay` (the rules display page does not autopause, +0xb4 cleared by `DE_CRulesDisplay::ObjInitContents` 0x35062ef0).
189. **Encompassing affectors** [Core `DE_CAffector::InternalIsTouching` 0x7007baa0, `IsTouching(results)` 0x7007b900, `ObjPrepare` 0x7007bf60, `AutoEncompase` 0x7007c360, `InternalAutoEncompase` 0x7007c400, `ObjProcessContents` 0x7007ae40, `ObjProcessReferences` 0x7007b000, `DE_CScene::Add` 0x7006ed50]: affector contents v3 carry three BOOLs after the groups (+0x138 lightable list, +0x16c may encompass, +0x170 auto-encompass); references v4 an owner (+0x114) and an encompasser (+0x120, target +0x124). When prepared, an affector with no encompasser and +0x170 set takes the first ancestor with +0x16c, or failing that the last such child of each ancestor in turn (`AutoEncompase`); it joins its encompasser's sub-affector list (+0x12c) and its groups are ORed into the encompasser's all-groups (+0x178, passed up the chain). The scene's affector list (+0x184) holds only affectors with no encompasser and some groups. `InternalIsTouching` tests a target whose all-groups match: one whose own groups (+0x174) match is tested into the results; otherwise it is tested into a throwaway result, and if touched its sub-affectors are tested in turn, the last first, stopping at the first touch when the results ask for one. In the data: each fighter's `EncompasingAffector` (Pelvis; own 0x30) holds its hit spheres and `NeedAffector`, each arena's `NavParent` (own 0) its navigation points, fighting surfaces and lights, Iceberg's force-field and jump-pad encompassers, Mantis's flies' `VisibilityAffector` their `Need` and `PickupDetector`. Over 2000-3000-frame fights in every arena the touched sets are the same as the flat walk's (the encompassers enclose their sub-affectors); the order of results changes, which needs walk from the last.
190. **Executors tick as the scene adds them; crowd response** [Core `DE_CScene::Add` 0x7006ed50, `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::DoneAddingExecutors` 0x70146450, `Tick` 0x70145700, setup (`m_ExecutorsNeedingInitialTick`, the class executor's +0x48), `DE_CClassExecutor::ObjInitContents` 0x701432c0 / `ObjProcessContents` 0x701434b0, `DE_CFlatText3::CenterHorizontal` 0x701cdf00 / `SetShadowOn` 0x701cde50 / `SetFont` 0x701ccd20, `TextSetShadowOnCaller` 0x7012ba80, `TextSetInGameFontCaller` 0x7012bbd0; Session `AddPropertyCaller` 0x45014ba0 / 0x45019e60; scripts `BText`, `BCrowdResponse`, `BCrowdResponseSlot`, `BStandardOMFSession`]: ClassExecutor contents v4/v5 add BOOLs +0x48 (default 1), +0x4c (and v5 +0x50, +0x54). An executor instance set up with +0x48 joins the initial-tick list, which the outermost `DE_CScene::Add` ticks on its way out, so its entry has run before `Add` returns; one without waits for its first tick. All shipped executors have it except Jaguar's lasers and laser hit effect, the arenas' own executors (BladePit, City, Iceberg, ...), FirePit's launchers, the cruise missile, mushroom cloud, fighter jet, lightning flasher and launch pads. So `BText`'s `Entry` (which makes the text primitive and clears the text) has run when `BCrowdResponseSlot` calls `CenterHorizontal`, `SetComplicatedFont`, `SetShadowOn` and `SetAsOverlay` through its interface, and they reach the primitive at once; we had deferred every `Scene().Add` entry to the end of the tick. The flat text's flags are independent of order (`CenterHorizontal` sets +0x50 bits, `SetShadowOn` +0x5c 0x80 with offset 0.06 and colour (0, 0, 0, 0.5), `SetInGameFont` the font name `FONT_IN_GAME_DEFAULT`). The crowd-response texts did not exist in ours at all: `BCrowdResponse` (the Crowd Response option, and the single-player tournaments) hands the slot modifier to the master with `Session().Master().AddProperty(g_CrowdSlotModifier)`, a native we lacked, so `BStandardOMFSession` never copied it into the slots; `AddProperty` appends to the modifier's property list.
191. **Each bound input manager keeps its own records** [Creature `DE_CJoystickInputManager::Get2DOrientation` 0x15001a20, `ButtonState` 0x15001ac0, `Tick` 0x150028d0, `InsertRecord` 0x15003050, `SetAsUserControlled` 0x150036f0; script `BObserverCamera` (`Tick`, `AssureActivated`)]: a manager bound to a device (+0x2c not -1) reads its 2D orientation and buttons from its own last record (+0xec, count +0xf0), which its `Tick` (every +0x10c = 16 ms of scene time) inserts when the device's state changes; unbound, the defaults. Binding does not clear the records. The observer's `UserController` (`SetAsUserControlled(0)` while the observer is active) is such a manager: `Is2DOrientationLeft/Right` see the keys as of its last tick, not live.
192. **`GatherAllObjectsDerivedFrom`'s order** [Core `GatherAllObjectsDerivedFromCaller` 0x7014d350, `DE_CTransferAgent::FindObjectsDerivedFrom` 0x7005b880, `FindDerivedFrom` 0x7005b340, `ProcessEvent` 0x700582c0 (case 9), `InternalFindObjectInfo` 0x70057bb0, `DE_CGUIDPool::Add` 0x7005d140, `DE_CTransferAgentWinFileFactory::Init` 0x70050300 (`FindFile` / `FindNextFileA`)]: the agent list is walked from its last agent back; each agent appends the GUIDs of its object infos (+0xe8, header order, searched linearly) whose factory derives from the class, skipping GUIDs already in the pool; then each GUID's master object is appended unless already there. The file factory makes an agent per data file as `FindNextFile` returns them, so the order is the directory listing's reversed: on NTFS, names compared upper-cased (inference: the install's file system is not known). The creature descriptions are one per robot file, so the single-player creature list before its shuffle runs Warlord, the Trophy Room's, Pyros, Mantis, Katana, Jaguar, Gargoyle, Force, Flyers, Chronos; ours had run by file name forwards, which changed which robots the shuffle deals (North American Open's first match: Force and Gargoyle instead of Pyros and Mantis).
193. **The camera at the end of a match won by the user: not reproduced** [scripts `BFighterStats` (`NotifyOfChange`, `AdjustHealthAndStun`, `FighterBuildStun`, `FighterStanding`), `BFighter` (`FighterProcessEvent`, `Ground.Initialize`, `Ground.Stunned`)]: `NotifyOfChange` sends `g_OMFFighterHealthExpended` (status dead) and then, if the fighter had been told of a stun and its stun resistance is at or above 0, `g_OMFFighterRecoverFromStun` (status normal). That can happen to a fighter killed while stunned once its stun resistance comes back (`Ground.Stunned` keeps calling `FighterBuildStun` each frame; `FighterStanding` restores it), and to one whose stun resistance is exactly 0 when it dies (both tests pass in one call). This is script behaviour, so the original would do the same in the same situation; whether a real killing hit leaves a fighter in one is not settled. With `OPENBG_KO` on North American Open's first match (both builds, two KOs to end the match) the defeated fighters stay at interest 0 and the observer watches the winner.
194. **Scene sounds wait for the scene watchers; `IsPaused` and the rules display** [Core `DE_CScene::PlayNoiseAmbient` 0x70072c20, `PlayNoiseSphere` 0x70072b70, `ActivateSceneWatchers` 0x7006f020 (+0x1b8), `ActivateSceneWatchersCaller`, `DE_GLOBAL` init (the global scene's watchers, then `DE_CAudioPlayer::CreateAudioPlayer`); scripts `BRulesDisplay` (`ActivatingSceneWatchers.Update`, `Done.Update`), `BScoreLimit` (`Entry`, `CheckAllSlotsForLimitReached`), `BAnnouncer`, `BCreatureAndPilotPicker`]: both noise players return nothing unless the scene holds an audio player whose +0x14c answers true; a session's scene gets the scene viewer's watchers, the audio player among them, only from `ActivateSceneWatchers(1)`, which only `BRulesDisplay` calls ("Activating Video and Audio"). So `BScoreLimit`'s first leader check (its leader starts at -1 and the session's at 0, so it always fires) announces `AnnounceLostTheLead(-1)` to an announcer whose focus slot is still -1, into a silent scene. In Practice the user's slot is not ready for action until the rules display closes, and the match cannot start before that, so `Done.Update` closes it on `Scene().TemporalMaster().IsPaused()`: entry 188's reading (paused only until `Genesis` at `OwnEnvironment`) leaves that false and deadlocks there; what keeps the temporal master paused while the rules display is up is not traced, and ours also counts a page's autopause as paused.
195. **`ScaleModifier` on a mesh** [Core `DE_CScaleModifier::Process` 0x7011e2b0 (instruction listing), `DE_CMesh::Process` 0x70127d30 / `ProcessProperties` 0x70128640 / `ObjProcessReferences` 0x701276e0, `DE_CStandardMesh::Process` 0x701303d0, `DE_CFlatDataElement::SetDataAsDefault` 0x7002f500 / `RestoreDataDefault` 0x7002f550, `DE_TCoord3D::Multiply` 0x7011ed10]: a mesh's references chunk (v1/v2, a BOOL-terminated list into +0x34) holds its modifiers, and `ProcessProperties` hands each the render mesh element after `StandardMesh::Process` has registered itself on the original positions, normals and face normals. The modifier registers only on `cOriginalPositionElement`: a static one once at priority 100000, a dynamic one every frame at 300000 (+0xe4 set, so its first run saves the positions as the element's default). On positions (type 4, 0x19..0x1b) it restores the default (dynamic only), then multiplies each by (+0x2c, +0x40, +0x54); no translation. Its normals branch (type 5: multiply by 1/s, 9999999 for 0, normalize) is never reached, as nothing registers it on a normal element, so the vertex normals stay the unscaled mesh's. In the data: Chronos's `Hedra02` crystals (`Size`), the fireball / spider / explosion / black-hole spheres and Katana's blade (each a plain FLOAT its behaviour sets with `SetFLOATValue` on the actor above), all dynamic and uniform; static ones on Iceberg's battleships (x2), `SharedObjects` axes and Volcano's `Glow` ring (1, 3, 1).
196. **Texture Detail picks the first mip level** [Direct3D `DE_CD3DTexture::CreateTextureFromImage` 0x2001bce0 (listing 0x2001be62..0x2001c028), `CreateTextureFromCubeImage` 0x2001c9f0, `DE_CRendererDirect3D::ApplyOptions` 0x200269f0; Core `DE_CImage::ObjProcessContents` 0x7018d850, `IsAdjustMipMapLevel` 0x70174900, `DE_CStandardImage::SetMipMapLevel` 0x70194f80 / `AssureMipMapImage` 0x70194c40]: when the image's `AdjustMipMapLevel` BOOL (+0x5c; contents v4+, after the mip-map BOOL +0x4c and a float +0x50) is set, D = options +0x1c (0 High, 1 Medium, 2 Low), else 0. The image is set to level D (`AssureMipMapImage` makes (W >> D) x (H >> D), failing and staying at full size when either is 0), the D3D texture is made at that size with a full chain (+0x4c) or one level, and D3D level L is filled from image level L + D. So Medium halves and Low quarters every adjusting texture: 1848 of the 2465 images, not the GUI's, Help's, pilots' or most core images. Cube images take no detail; an AVI's `SetMipMapLevel` is `DE_CImage`'s (true only for 0), so AVIs keep their size. `ApplyOptions` deletes every render texture when +0x1c (or +8) changed, so the next draw rebuilds them at the new level. `DE_CStandardRenderTexture::CreateTextureData` (0x20012580, FINDINGS 180) caps a base level from the image's vfunc +0x180 the same way, but the Direct3D path above is the one that sizes the textures.
197. **Light projector polygons, cell by cell** [Direct3D `DE_CD3DLight::UpdateTouchedPolygons` 0x2002cd00, `DE_CD3DRenderMeshElement::AssureProjectedTargetOctree` 0x2002e1f0 / `FillProjectedVertexBuffer` 0x2002e4f0, `DE_CStandardRenderAffector::Update` 0x20006570; Core `DE_COctree::ObjProcessContents` 0x702051c0, `CIndexNode::ProcessContents` 0x702060e0, `Create` 0x70207010, `CNode::AssureMaximumSize` 0x70205450, `GetPotentialSphereCollisions` 0x702064e0, `CIndexNode::GetPotentialCollisions` 0x70205be0 / 0x70205e60, `DE_CConeAffector::IsTouchingSphere` 0x70080560 (listing)]:
   - **Octree.** Each projector-target element keeps an octree in mesh space: the mesh object's EX `D3DProjectedTargetOctree` when it has one (54 meshes, the arenas' big ones: chunk v2/v3, a name block, then per node six floats (box), eight BOOL-prefixed children, a leaf BOOL with an i32 count and u32 face ids; v3 adds six squeezed counts and three floats), else `Create` with cells of at most 100 (40 without a render affector): the vertices' box, split at its centre along every axis longer than that and at least half the longest, no deeper than six levels; a leaf holds the faces whose bounds touch its box, every node the union below it. The node radius is half its box's diagonal.
   - **Query.** A sphere light (render affector type 7) takes its centre into mesh space and gathers the nodes its bounding box (centre +- falloff, +0x11c = +0x17c) touches, a node whole once it is a leaf or its radius is below the falloff. A cone gathers the leaves whose bounding spheres (centre through the actor's frame) its affector's `IsTouchingSphere` accepts: |c - apex| <= r + far end, z - r <= far end, z + r >= near end (+0x188), and radial < z tan(penumbra) or radial - (1 + tan) r - |z tan| < 0.
   - **Drawing.** `FillProjectedVertexBuffer` copies the three positions of each gathered face into the light's own buffer, so a projector pass draws each touched polygon once per light, whatever the material's pass count (we drew every pass of every batch the light's reach overlapped, adding twice over two-pass materials).
198. **Alpha-blended image projectors: intensity is alpha** [Direct3D `DE_CStandardProjectorShader::SetupConeProjector` 0x20034540 / 0x20034750, `SetStates` 0x20033830, `DrawPatches` 0x20033b20, `DE_CShader::CStage::SetStates` 0x20031b60]: stage fields +0x24 COLOROP, +0x2c COLORARG2, +0x30 ALPHAOP, +0x34 ALPHAARG2 (ARG1 is always TEXTURE, ALPHAREPLICATE on flag 0x20). The cone stages: 0 the image, MODULATE by TFACTOR, alpha SELECTARG1; 1 the core `ShadowAttenuation`, MODULATE by CURRENT, alpha SELECTARG1 (A8R8G8B8: its alpha channel equals its luminance ramp). For blends 1 and 5 (SRCALPHA / INVSRCALPHA) `SetStates` makes both alpha ops MODULATE (stage 0 by TFACTOR, stage 1 by CURRENT), so alpha = image alpha x TFACTOR alpha x ramp. `DrawPatches` sets TFACTOR to the light's colour x intensity with alpha 1, except when `SetupConeProjector` cleared +0x68 (exactly blends 1 and 5): then the colour unscaled and the alpha the intensity, each clamped. So a scorch mark (black, blend 1) fades by `BScorchMark`'s `SetIntensity` ramps through its alpha; with the intensity on the colour it stayed at full darkness until deleted.
199. **`TestLineSegment` against a box: flat boxes only, and none is** [Core `DE_CBoxAffector::IsTouchingCone` 0x7007e760, `IsTouchingConeAsLineSegment` 0x7007e7a0, `InternalWorldUpdate` 0x7007eee0, `ObjInitContents` 0x7007e020, `ObjProcessContents` 0x7007e260, `DE_CPlane3d::IsLineIntersect` 0x7000cf90, `DE_TCoord3D<float>::MakeNormal` 0x7022ba40, `DE_CSingleCollisionInfo::SetTouchPoint` 0x7007f730, `DE_CScene::TestLineSegment` 0x700731b0, `DE_CCoordinateSystem::Set` 0x70025fe0; `DE_CConeAffector::IsTouchingBox` 0x70080c70 / `IsTouchingCone` 0x70080c60, `DE_CPyramidAffector::IsTouchingCone` 0x70088140, `DE_CAffector::IsTouchingCone` 0x7007d490]: a box answers a cone only with penumbra 0, through its plane list (+0x1a0, count +0x1a4). `InternalWorldUpdate` fills it only for a box whose min z equals its max z: one plane through the world corners (min x, min y), (min x, max y), (max x, max y) at that z, and the quad's local corners at +0x1ac (x) / +0x1bc (y) in that order; any other box keeps no plane and is never touched by a line segment. For a flat box the line through the cone's apex along its z axis (the unit segment direction) meets the plane at any parameter, before the start or past the end (`IsLineIntersect` has no bounds, only a parallel check); the point, taken into the box's frame, must pass a crossing-number test of its local x, y against the four corners (edge i from corner i-1, half-open in y, strictly left in x). One collision is appended (cone, box) with only the touch point (when the results want touch points): no normal, distance or depth, no `ReverseResults`. Cones and pyramids never answer a cone. In the data no box is flat (each arena's death volume, danger boxes, Iceberg's force fields and their encompassers, Sport's launch pads and score box, Volcano's chargers and lava), so segments touch no box, and a box encompasser's sub-affectors are never reached by a segment. Ours used a slab test, which had an Iceberg force field stop a segment. The plane is the one from the box's last world update; ours uses its current frame.
200. **What lets Practice's rules display close: not traced to a pause** [Core `IsPausedCaller` 0x7006de90, `TemporalMasterCaller` 0x7006de70, `AssureTemporalMasterCaller` 0x7006dda0 / `DE_CScene::AssureTemporalMaster` 0x70072a00, `Genesis` 0x70070900, `EnsureGenesisCaller` 0x7006dcf0, `DE_CTemporalMasterScene::ObjInitContents` 0x700787a0 / `BeginSceneUpdate` 0x70078b30 / `TemporalPause` 0x70078850 / `TemporalContinue` 0x70078890 / `TemporalGlobalTime` 0x70078910, `DE_CScene::TemporalUpdate` 0x70070710, `DE_CSceneViewerControl3d::EnsureSceneTimeStarted` 0x700c2260 / `AttachScene` 0x700c19f0, `DE_CAppManager::IsUIManagerAutopaused` 0x7019b260 / `CloseUIManagerStack` 0x7019bd00 / `SetNextUIManager` 0x7019af60 / `IsUIManagerHaltingLoading` 0x7019b200, `DE_CUIManager::SetDescriptionChosen` 0x701eb5c0 / `UIManagerComplete` 0x701eb320 / `RemoveFromStack` 0x701eba60 (empty), `DE_CSession::Update` 0x701f8ab0 / `IsServer` 0x70075750; Session `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` 0x4500ecf0 / `Genesis` 0x4500f2a0 / `IsReadyToLoad` 0x4500f810, `DE_CStandardSessionModifier::OwnEnvironment` 0x450161e0; OMF `DE_CRulesDisplay::Begin` 0x350630f0, `DE_CChooseFighterUIManagerRobot::ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x35057270; scripts `BRulesDisplay`, `BSimpleRequestDelay`, `BWaitFor1Fighter`, `BCreatureAndPilotPicker`, `BEnvironmentPicker`, `BSinglePlayerSubSession`]: the temporal master (`TemporalMaster()` is the scene's +0x140, the scene itself until `AssureTemporalMaster`) gets +0xf0 = 1 from `DE_CScene::ObjInitContents`, and only `Genesis` and `Continue` clear it. A standard session that is the server makes the master in `Begin` (and `BSinglePlayerSubSession`'s `Entry` asks again), before its environment; `BEnvironmentPicker` owns the environment while `BRulesDisplay` loads (`SessionLoadElements`), and `OwnEnvironment` → `DE_CStandardSession::Genesis` → the scene viewer's (the one `Begin` attached the scene to) `EnsureSceneTimeStarted` → `DE_CScene::Genesis`, which on the scene's first call passes `Genesis` on to the master (vtable +0x138 resolved). `TemporalPause` / `TemporalContinue` drive +0x1d8 (time lag), not +0xf0; `Pause` (+0x144) has no caller found besides the model picker's robot. So by `Done.Update` the master reads not paused, and `Done` should close on `StandardSessionIsMatchStarted`. In ours the match never starts because the scene passes no time: `BWaitFor1Fighter` (a `BSimpleRequestDelay`) checks the slots only once scene time has moved, and Choose Creature (+0xb4 autopause, as `DE_CUIManager::ObjInitContents` sets) is on the UI stack under the rules display: `DE_CRulesDisplay::Begin` calls `SetNextUIManager(this, this, 1)`, which pushes the current page, and `AssureRulesDisplay`'s `CloseUIManagerStack` does not clear the current page (its final `SetNextUIManager(0)` only queues the switch). The OK button's `SetDescriptionChosen` runs the complete hooks with the page still current, so as read, the original also pushes Choose Creature and `IsUIManagerAutopaused` (when the newest session is, or hangs under, a server) would stop the scene's time (`TemporalUpdate`, unless scene +0x1bc / +0x1c0). Which step differs in the original (the newest-session test in `IsUIManagerAutopaused`, frame timing of the page switch, or something else) is not settled, so ours keeps counting a page's autopause as paused (`World::scene_paused`); without it Practice stalls at "Activating Video and Audio".
201. **A live creature takes back its own state registered between its ticks** [Creature `DE_CCreature::Tick` 0x15016870, `UnpackState` 0x15015160, `SetTime` 0x15015d20, `RegisterExecutorState` 0x15015650, `ObjPrepare` 0x15016050; Core `DE_CStandardStateManager::CreateCurrentState` 0x701aeea0, `DE_CStateManager::AddCurrentState` 0x70126250 / `AddToStateList` 0x701b19f0 / `GetState` 0x701b1f40, `DE_CScene::TemporalUpdate` 0x70070710 / `Resimulate`; scripts `BFighter` (`ObjectSpawn`, `Air.FallFromSpawn`, `StandardGetHit*`), `BFighterStats` (`NotifyOfChange`)]: CreateCurrentState puts every `Register*State` into the manager's state list (+0x3c) whether or not the session records (only a replay-driven manager, +0x78, or one with a fixup pending makes none), stamped with the owner's local time, a tick-based creature's +0x1d4. Tick, unless held (+0x2fc 2), with +0x2fc 0, a state manager, a first tick behind it (+0x20c) and +0x1d4 not the state being unpacked (+0x214), asks `GetState(+0x1d4)` (an exact time; for a replay-driven manager after `UpdateForReplay` of that time) and restores the state through `SetupOwnerForState`, skipping physics and the behaviour executor that tick. A creature's own brain registers inside its tick, before +0x1d4 is incremented, so those states carry a tick already gone; a state registered after the creature's tick and before its next one (from another creature's or a collision tester's tick calling into it, an event, the session update) carries the coming tick and is taken back there: the executors restart from the registration point (`ProcessStatus`: frames rebuilt, sleep cleared, nothing run), position, orientation and physics are set back to then, and the tick's physics and brain are lost. In a fight these are the spawn (`ObjectSpawn` from the spawner enters `Air.FallFromSpawn`, which registers a get-hit state: "register the state, wait a frame, then fall"), the `StandardGetHit*` reactions and `GroundBlock` / counter-block states entered from the attacker's hit test, and the FighterStats creature's (rate 100) `NotifyOfChange` when damage comes in; 17 to 49 such restores in four 3000-frame AI fights. `UnpackState` zeroes +0x19c, so the creature-state-changed hooks (+0x1b8, `RegisterCreatureStateChangedHook`, e.g. `BFighter`'s `FighterChangedState`) run at the end of that tick, as after every change of the behaviour executor's state method (+0xf0); a fighter (+0x1e0) then defers its `SetTime` to the scene's next `TemporalUpdate`, where it re-ticks only a clock left behind. Scene +0x158 is set only by `Resimulate` and around `TemporalUpdate`'s deferred `SetTime`s; a creature tick (and delta tick) leaves its command manager alone while it is set.
202. **No shipped creature is delta-ticked** [Creature `DE_CCreature::ObjPrepare` 0x15016050, `DeltaTick` 0x15016700, `ObjProcessContents` 0x15014820, `DE_CStateManager::GetNewestStateInRange` 0x1501a630 / `GetStateOrPrevious` 0x1501a510; Core `DE_CScene::PassTime` 0x70070040, `AddDeltaTick` 0x7000f910, `DE_CPhysics::UpdateEnabled`]: ObjPrepare puts a creature whose +0x1e0 (the second u32 of its contents v2-v4) is set into the scene's SetTime list (+0x84) and, when it has no tick rate (+0x1dc 0), into the delta-tick list (+0x70), which `PassTime` runs after the delta-tick hooks; nothing else adds a creature there (`AddDeltaTick` has no callers; physics add themselves). After its first run, `DeltaTick` restores the newest state its manager holds after +0x1e4 and at or before the scene time (`GetNewestStateInRange`, after `UpdateForReplay(now)` when replay-driven), delta-ticks the command manager unless re-simulating, looks up its behaviour executor and runs the state-changed hooks, then sets +0x1e4 and +0x1d4 to the scene time. Of the 223 `Actor\Creature` objects in the data, those with +0x1e0 (the fighters, eleven projectiles, EnergyBoost, FighterJetCarryable, PowerPlant's platforms and lightning wall) all have a tick rate, so the delta tick never runs with the shipped data.
203. **Executors have no `SetTime`** [Core `DE_CScene::PassTime` 0x70070040, `DE_CReferenceTarget::SetTime` 0x70006560 (`ret 4`); vtable +0x48 of `DE_CClassExecutorInstance` (0x702675ec), `DE_CClassExecutor`, `DE_CCollisionTester`, `DE_CSimplePhysics`, `DE_CMomentumMonitor`; Creature `DE_CCommandExecutor`, `DE_CStandardHookupExecutor`, `DE_CDigitalOrientationPropulsionExecutor`, `DE_CMouseAxisNavigationExecutor`, `DE_CTeleportExecutor`, `DE_CThrusterExecutor`, `DE_CCommandManager`, `DE_CJoystickInputManager`]: on a clock jump (back, or a replay's pass over 2000 ms) `PassTime` restarts each temporal set at its last tick at or before the new time (+0x30 = t / rate, +0x38 = that x rate) and calls `SetTime` (+0x48) on its elements, newest first, then on the +0x84 list. Every executor class, the class executor instances included, inherits the empty `DE_CReferenceTarget::SetTime`, as do collision testers, simple physics, command and input managers; only `DE_CCreature` (0x15015d20) overrides it. A jump does nothing to an executor beyond its set's restart: its next tick comes a rate after the restarted base, and its sleeps, which count ticks, carry on.
204. **A creature with no tick rate keeps its local time at +0x1d4** [Creature `DE_CCreature::LocalTime` 0x15008300, `RegisterExecutorState` 0x15015650, `ObjInitContents`, `ObjPrepare` 0x15016050, `UnpackState` 0x15015160, `SetTime` 0x15015d20, `DeltaTick` 0x15016700]: with no tick rate, `LocalTime` and the stamp of its states are the scene time only when its ID flags (+0x1d0) have 0x80; otherwise +0x1d4, which ObjInitContents zeroes and only ObjPrepare (when the creation time is already past), `UnpackState`, `SetTime` and `DeltaTick` write. A no-rate creature made at run time keeps +0x1d4 at 0 until a state is restored, so all its states are stamped 0, each replacing the last in the history (none ages out), and the record gets its last one; on playback `UpdateForReplay(0)` takes that state at once, after which `SetTime` puts +0x1d4 at the scene time and nothing newer exists. Twenty shipped no-rate creatures have a state manager; those without 0x80 include Canyon's GunBase and GunTrigger (0x1c), the LauncherActivateActorSpawners (0x56), Iceberg's Launcher, PowerPlant's PickupSpawner, ServerMonitorCamera and the single-player power-ups (0x14).
205. **Creature-state-changed hooks run at the end of a creature's tick** [Creature `DE_CCreature::Tick` 0x15016870, `DeltaTick` 0x15016700, `UnpackState` 0x15015160, `RegisterCreatureStateChangedPCHook` 0x15019ce0, `g_macUnregisterCreatureStateChangedPCHookCaller` 0x15014350, `CallCreatureStateChangedPCHook` 0x15011570, `ForceStateChangeCaller` 0x150140e0; Core `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::SetState` 0x701458f0 / 0x70145920, `ResumeExecution` 0x70144a80, `ProcessStatus` 0x70145b50, `ExecuteInstant` 0x70145860, `DE_CPCExecuter::Execute` 0x7014e3e0, `ContinueExecutionInternal` 0x7014eaa0 (ops 0x22 / 0x24, the frame return); scripts `BFighter` (`FighterChangedState`), `BPickup` (`PickupStateChanged`), `BKatana` (`Ground.SuperRisingBlade`)]: the hooks (+0x1b8, count +0x1bc) are `DE_CPCodeHook`s holding a weak instance and a method. Register searches from the end: a slot whose instance is gone is reused, an identical hook stops the search, otherwise one is appended; Unregister removes the last match. They are never run at the switch itself: at the end of `Tick` (after the state restore or the physics and behaviour executor, before the hold check and the +0x1d4 increment) and of `DeltaTick`, when the behaviour executor (+0x1a0) has a state (+0xf0) other than +0x19c, +0x19c takes it and every hook runs in registration order through `ExecuteInstant` with no arguments (skipping one whose instance has no owner, +0x18); several switches within one tick run them once, and a switch back to the same state not at all. `Tick` returns before them when its creature is destroyed during the brain's tick (+0x108). +0xf0 is the state last entered (SetState from an instant method's call or an interface, a nested state call, `Execute` of the default state or entry), returned to (a state frame), or restored (`ProcessStatus`: the innermost state frame); +0x19c is zeroed by ObjInitContents, `UnpackState` (so a restored creature runs them at the end of the tick that restores it, or of its next tick when restored from outside) and `ForceStateChange` (no shipped script calls it). The first tick after a fighter's entry runs `FighterChangedState`; from then on it resets traction, the danger areas, the AI's attack likelihood and jump button, and the interest of a fighter not dead, after every state change, one frame into the new state: Katana's `Ground.SuperRisingBlade.Leap` now starts at traction 1 instead of the 0.3 its launch set each frame. In six 3000-frame AI fights the hooks are the only difference from before (identical runs with them switched off); the fights part 740 to 1890 frames in.
206. **Temporal order within a frame; the wings** [Core `DE_CScene::Add` 0x7006ed50, `AddTemporal` 0x70071e80, `RemoveTemporal` 0x70071f10, `TemporalElementSetForRate` 0x70072230, `GetNextTemporalSet` 0x7006fbf0, `PassTime` 0x70070040, `DE_CTemporalElementSet::AdvanceTime` 0x70072090, `DE_CActor::ObjPrepare` 0x70090de0 / `InternalPrepareActive` 0x70090ca0, `DE_CClassExecutor::ObjPrepare` 0x70143840, `DE_CClassExecutorInstance::Setup` 0x70144df0, `DoneAddingExecutors` 0x70146450; Creature `DE_CCreature::ObjPrepare` 0x15016050]: sets are kept in creation order and the one whose next tick (+0x38 + rate) is lowest runs, the first made on a tie. A set appends a new element (one already in it keeps its place) and marks itself dirty; AdvanceTime copies the elements only when dirty and runs the copy from the last to the first, so what joins during a pass waits for the next. `Add` prepares an actor, then adds its active children from the last: a creature joins its set in its ObjPrepare before `DE_CActor::ObjPrepare`, whose InternalPrepareActive prepares the properties in order, a ClassExecutor's Setup joining the executor's interval set at once (before any entry runs); `DoneAddingExecutors` then ticks the initial-tick list in Setup order. We registered all of a branch's creatures first and its executors after, in breadth-first order, and started entries in that order; now each actor's executors follow it and the entries run in Setup order. The fights do not change (the six AI fights are identical); the main menu's buttons join the 100 ms set in the other order. For the Gargoyle the 16 ms set holds the fighter, then (as its branch is added) its controller's input manager and WingBase's BWings executor: BWings ticks before the fighter in every pass, so on a frame where both write a wing actor BGargoyle's `AnimSetTime` (validated at once) is the last write.
207. **The camera at the end of a match won by the user, with the hooks** [scripts `BFighter` (`Ground.Death`, `FighterChangedState`), `BFighterStats` (`NotifyOfChange`)]: `Ground.Death` loops in one state, so the hooks run for a defeated fighter only on a restore or a real state change; `FighterChangedState` raises its interest only while its status is not dead, and then `Ground.Death` gets it up (`Ground.GetUpFaceUp`) while its state is alterable, so a 0/1 flip would need a revived fighter, which is the scripts' behaviour. With `OPENBG_KO=3200,4600` on North American Open's first match the defeated fighters stay at interest 0 after the second KO and the observer watches the winner alone, as before the hooks; between rounds every fighter goes back to 1. The flip was not reproduced.
208. **Why Choose Creature does not stall Practice's match: `IsDisplayed`** [Core `DE_CAppManager::IsUIManagerAutopaused` 0x7019b260, `SetNextUIManager` 0x7019af60, `CloseUIManagerStack` 0x7019bd00, `TransitionUIManager` 0x7019ab30, `IsDisplayedCaller`, `IsActive` 0x7019aeb0, `DE_CTopControl3d::StartModal` / `StopModal`, `DE_CScene::ObjInitContents` 0x7006e8d0 / `TemporalUpdate` 0x70070710; Session `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` 0x4500ecf0; OMF `DE_CRulesDisplay::Begin` 0x350630f0; script `BRulesDisplay` (`Update`, `AssureRulesDisplay`)]: `IsUIManagerAutopaused` is true while `DE_CControl3d::s_ModalControl` is set (a top control in `DoModal`: any modal dialog, the notification among them); otherwise, with a page current or stacked, false when the newest session is a remote client's (its owner chain's root has no server number +0x98; `SetAsServer` numbers from 1), else true if any stacked page or the current one has +0xb4. `TemporalUpdate` applies it only to scenes with +0x1c0 and +0x1bc clear: `ObjInitContents` sets +0x1c0, and only `DE_CStandardSession::Begin` clears it, on its own scene, so temporal masters and GUI scenes never autopause. `BRulesDisplay`'s base `Update` (the `WaitingToBegin` state has none of its own) calls `AssureRulesDisplay` every frame until `m_RulesDisplay.IsDisplayed()`: `CloseUIManagerStack` (each stacked page made next and removed, then `SetNextUIManager(0)`, which finishes the current page), then `Begin` → `SetNextUIManager(this, this, 1)`, which first clears +0x78 and returns at once when the display is already current, so it un-finishes it. While Choose Creature is still current (its doors closing) each call stacks it again; once the transition has made the rules display current the next call removes it from the stack and stacks nothing. `IsDisplayedCaller` is true without controls (`AreControlsEnabled`), else when the page has activated (+0xa0) and `PageTransitionBusy` is false, so at least one such call follows the switch while the doors open. Ours had answered `IsDisplayed` with "Begin was called", left `WaitingToBegin` before the switch and kept Choose Creature stacked, hence the stall (FINDINGS 200's question). `IsActiveLocally` is `IsActive`: current and (transition busy or begun +0x9c), or next, or a child UI manager active.
209. **Choose Creature's robot is a bare FighterTemplate** [OMF `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManagerRobot` ctor 0x350551b0, `UpdateModel` 0x35055a10, `ProcessIdleEvent` 0x350559f0, `ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x35055890, `DE_CFighterModelDescription::NewStrippedFighterModel` 0x35038940, `DE_CCreatureModel::Setup` 0x35037470 (`DE_CCreatureModel` vtable +0x118)]: the constructor keeps a construct of `LoadScene\FighterTemplate` (+0x98) and calls the same code as `UpdateModel`, which every arrow click runs (including the wrap): pause the picker scene (`Pause`, continued by the next idle event), `DeleteThisLater` the old robot (+0x94), set the name label, copy the template (`ObjNewCopy(1)`), parent `LastSelectedPilot()`'s resource to it, build the stripped model (a new `DE_CCreatureModel` holding a new instance of the model description's +0x5c `FighterModel`, texture managers' images replaced by name from +0xe4) under it, call the model's `Setup(robot)` (vtable +0x118, the same hookup that gives the robot's `FighterImageFilter` the model's `RGBMain` / `ReflectionMask` / `RGBScaled` images, FINDINGS 165, so the pilot paints it), parent the robot to `RobotPedistal` (+0x90) and add it to the picker's scene. The template (an `OMF2Fighter` at the identity under its parent) has no skeleton (its `Pelvis` has no children), no `ActorDeformInfo`, no `AnimationSequenceManager` and no executors (its properties are a state manager, joystick input manager, command manager and fighter physics), so the model is drawn undeformed (its bind pose) on the pedestal, turning with it. The data's model descriptions carry no replacement images that we found.
210. **The notification dialog, decoded** [Core `DE_CDialog3d::UserInformation` 0x700f32b0 (disassembly), `DE_CButtonOverlay::MouseEnter` 0x7009b340 / `MouseLeave` 0x7009b390, `DE_CBevelPanelControlInterface3d::SetColors` 0x700c3410 / `SetCornerColors` 0x700c3270, `DE_CDialog3d::Center` 0x700f1d30; checked against the original's screenshot at 1024 × 768 (upscaled)]: frame VPU 4; a down panel (VPU 3) filled with the interface's window back at alpha 0.7; with an icon, a column `OffsetFromLeftWithLen(0, VPU 75)` ending VPU 60 above the bottom, holding a 64 × 64 VPU box (alignment 6, fill white 0.8, the image); lines measured at VPU 14, kept when within VPU 300 − 75, else wrapped at VPU 400 / 600 / 700 − 75 (total / width ≤ 2, ≤ 8, else), each a text control in a row `OffsetFromTopWithLen(10 + i × trunc(1.5 h), trunc(1.5 h))` from the column's right, centred; OK a plain button (15-VPU label) in a box `LenY` VPU 38, `OffsetFromRightWithLen(VPU 10, VPU 120)`, alignment 2, inside a bottom strip. Size: width = the dialog's frame extents + 75 + widest line (at least VPU 300 − 75) + 10, height = max(180 px, extents + VPU 60 + text bottom + 5), centred. `SetColors` takes the five sections in the order top, bottom, left, right, face (ids 1-5); the overlay's hover array is top A A B B, bottom B B A A, left A B B A, right B A A B, face B with A = (0, 0, 1, 0.4), B = (0, 0, 1, 0), on a frame VPU 8 wide, while the box's fill goes from black 0.2 to clear; `MouseLeave` sets the frame alpha back to 0. The strip's length comes from a double `ConvertToVPU` whose input we could not pin down; the screenshot puts the box's centre VPU 50 above the panel's bottom (a VPU 100 strip). `re/specs/controls.md`'s icon column "VPU 60" is that column's bottom offset; its width is VPU 75.
211. **Which meshes take scene lights** [Direct3D `DE_CStandardRenderActor::AssureLightable` 0x20004450, `IsLightable` 0x20004440, `JoinStandardRenderActorLightableAffector` 0x200037c0, `HookHierarchy` / `Reset` 0x20003c70, `DE_CStandardRenderer::AddActor` / `RemoveActor`, `MarkVisibleLights` 0x2000aad0, `DE_CStandardRenderLight::UpdateLitActors` 0x2000f720, `DE_CGeometryPatch::ActivateLights` 0x2001ebe0, `DE_CD3DRenderMeshElement::InitialSetup` 0x2002e550, `IsLightable` 0x2002f6a0 (vtable +0xf0), `SetLightElement` 0x2002f8f0 (+0x118), `RunProcess` 0x2002f3e0; Core `DE_CActor::ObjSetParent` 0x7008afb0, `Attach` 0x70091c30; script `BPickupSpawner` (`AssurePickupsCreated`)]: a mesh element reaches lights only through its render actor's lightable affector (+0x48). `SetAffector` joins a lightable render affector (groups 0x20) to its owner's render actor at any time; otherwise `InitialSetup` of a vertex- or projector-lit element calls `AssureLightable`, which takes the first +0x48 up the render actor tree (+0x2c) and keeps it. That tree is hooked once, in `AddActor`, under the actor's parent actor (+0xf8) as it is when the actor enters the scene; `ObjSetParent` re-adds an actor only when its new parent is in another scene, and otherwise just notifies the render actor (`RefNotifyInvalidateTarget` -> `MirrorInvalidated`, which copies +0x108), so reparenting changes neither the tree nor +0x48; leaving the scene deletes the render actor (`Reset` clears both). Every lit path needs +0x48: `ActivateLights` sets the element's lights from it and disables the other D3D lights (so an element without one is lit by the ambient alone), the projector passes draw only for render affectors a light's `UpdateLitActors` listed, and `MarkVisibleLights`' software light elements (cLightElement, made only for meshes with a lighting process) are gated by vtable +0xf0 `IsLightable`. In the data: projectiles are passive references with placement 1 and no parent (FINDINGS 96), so they take no lights unless they own a lightable affector themselves (Jaguar's `ProjectileActor` owns `LightingAffector`); sparks, landing and other effects enter at the scene root and take none; pickups are `SetParent` to their spawner before `Scene().Add`, so they inherit the arena's (`VisibilityAffector`, owned by the arena root) and keep it when a fighter carries them (FINDINGS 110's "pickups have none" is wrong). Ours looked the owner up the current tree every frame, so a carried pickup took the fighter's lights, the arena's static spotlights among them; it now fixes an inherited owner when the actor is first drawn in the scene.
212. **What made screenshot runs differ, and SystemTime** [Core `SystemTimeCaller` 0x70046540, `gTime` 0x7002a730, `DE_CTransferAgent::FindMasterObject(GUID)` 0x7005bc20, `InternalFindObjectInfo` 0x70057bb0; DE_GLOBAL `SystemInit`]: `SystemTime()` is `gTime`, milliseconds of QueryPerformanceCounter (else `timeGetTime`) since its first call: wall-clock time, not the scene's. `BRulesDisplay` uses it for its 1000 / 1500 ms loading re-checks. Ours returned the scene time; it now returns the wall clock in normal play and 20 ms per frame in screenshot and headless runs. A GUID lookup asks the exported infos, then the file agents from the last one back, each answering with its first object of that GUID; the agents are in the data directory's order (FINDINGS 192), so where two files share a GUID (Iceberg and FirePit's `DeathMaterial` and `WaterVolume`, Pyros and FirePit's fire passes, Grudge Match Arena and BladePit's flares, Pickups and Canyon's spawner classes) the later name wins. Ours took the first in APFS's directory order. The rest was ours: the many maps and sets keyed by `GameObject` pointers iterate in address order, and the deque blocks holding the objects came from malloc, whose order differed between runs (about one run in six, North American Open's launch filled its scene a frame later, and from there the robots drew a different `rand()` sequence); the objects now come from one reserved range in creation order. `rand()` draws for a noise's alternates and frequency variance were made only when a sound device was open.
213. **The official patches, 2.090 → 2.123** [`PATCHES.md`; `re/scripts/rtpatch_emu.py`, `rtpatch_chain.sh`, `build_number.py`, `deobj_diff.cpp`, `patch_diff.py`, `pe_funcdiff.py`, `launcher_diff.py`; OMF `DE_COMF2Manager::Activate`, Core `WinSystemInit`]: the disc is 2.090 (`Setup/data1.hdr` product version 0.20.2090; the build number compiled into OMF's `Activate` and Core's `WinSystemInit` is 2090). The eight RTPatch 7.00 patches were applied by running the game's own `AutoPatch/patchw32.dll` under Unicorn (never natively); RTPatch verifies every old and new file, so `source/patched/v2095`, `v2096`, `v2097`, `v2101`, `v2112`, `v2123` are byte-exact. The files are mislabelled: "2097 → 2100" yields build 2101, "2101 → 2111" yields 2112 and "2112 → 2121" yields 2123 (the "2111 → 2112" and "2121 → 2123" patches find every entry already new), so the missing 2.100 → 2.101 patch loses nothing, and 2.100, 2.111 and 2.121 never exist on their own. No patch adds or deletes a file; each replaces the still-SafeDisc-wrapped launcher whole. Headlines: 2.095 jump-evades, side grapples, stun / block retuning and shared death volumes; 2.096 "delay on miss" for projectiles, server-authoritative hits and deaths, a `CAttackInfo` NoCounter flag, module rebase; 2.097 two null checks in the controls page; 2.101 Chronos / Gargoyle / Katana retuning, Redemption unlocks Raven, admin reset, UI-stack close fix; 2.112 a Light attack-type bit (damage < 15, later bits renumbered), unblockable attacks, the single-player Starting Match chooser, damage-scaled get-hit sounds, a new `DE_CObject::ObjFindValueByName` virtual; 2.123 get-hit debris scaled by `CActor::RenderQuality()`, front-end iris doors, observer camera retuning. Most of it is script and data the port runs as shipped; the engine-side list is in PATCHES.md.
214. **Attack info in 2.096-2.123: NoCounter, Light, unblockable attacks, the get-hit packet** [OMF 2.123 `SetNoCounterCaller` 0x10713210, `IsNoCounterCaller` 0x10713160, `IsSpecialCaller` 0x10713120, `IsHazardCaller` 0x107131a0, `StartSimpleAttackCaller` 0x107133f0 / `DE_CAttackInfo::StartSimpleAttack` 0x10717570, `AddAttackType*Caller` / `IsAttackType*Caller` 0x10713240..0x107133d0, `SetupExplosiveProjectileCaller` 0x10712710, `SetupExplosiveAttackCaller` 0x107127e0, `TestForFighterBlock` 0x107177d0 / 0x10717920, `AttackInfoNewCopyInstanceCaller` 0x10713b30, `PackForGetHitEffect` 0x10716fe0, `UnpackForGetHitEffect` 0x10717110]: NoCounter is bit 0x80 of the flags byte (+0xc4). Only its two callers touch it; the copy and the packet carry the whole byte, and the counters' scripts test it. `StartSimpleAttack` resets the attack as before, sets damage = stun = arg0 and recovery = arg1, and ORs Light (4) into the type when arg0 < 15.0. The type bits are now Standard 2, Light 4, Projectile 8, Cold 0x10, Heat 0x20, Electrical 0x40, Gravitational 0x80, TimeStop 0x100, NoReplicate 0x80000000; Light has only `IsAttackTypeLight`. The engine's own uses of the bits are the reset (2), `StartSimpleAttack`, and `SetupExplosiveProjectile` / `SetupExplosiveAttack`, which write 8; the packet, `GenerateContents` and the copy move the word whole. `TestForFighterBlock` returns "not blocked" when the attacker (+0x14) has the BOOL value `UnblockableAttacks`, after the null and is-a-fighter checks (a non-fighter defender still blocks). `UnpackForGetHitEffect` sets the damage to byte / 255 × 50. The port has no `SetupExplosive*` yet (none of our test fights reaches them). Flags 2 (`IsSpecial`) and 0x40 (`IsHazard`) have callers that no script uses.
215. **Fighter model descriptions** [OMF 2.123 `DE_CFighterDescription::GatherFighterModels` 0x10733a70, `GetRandomFighterModelDescription` 0x10733be0 / caller 0x10733600, `NewResource(DE_CGUID&)` 0x10733930; Core `MATH::Rand(int)` 0x200296d0; script `BCreatureAndPilotPicker`]: `GatherFighterModels`, a stub on the disc, collects every FighterModelDescription master object (`FindMasterObjectsDerivedFrom`) whose fighter GUID (+0xf0) is this description's GUID, and returns the index of the one with +0xd8 set. `GetRandomFighterModelDescription` picks `MATH::Rand(n)` = (rand() × n) >> 15 of them (null when there are none). The picker stores that description's own GUID as the chosen model when none was chosen, and `NewResource(GUID)` takes a FighterModelDescription's GUID as standing for its model (vtable +0x120), deleting the constructed description; any other GUID is the model itself.
216. **CanStateInterrupt** [Core 2.123 `DE_CStandardStateManager::AddLaggedStateDestIndex` 0x201b4e10 (disc 0x701ae2b0), `CheckForDiscardState` 0x201b4fb0; OMF `DE_COMF2Fighter::CanStateInterrupt` 0x1072aad0; `DE_CActor::CanStateInterrupt` returns 0]: walking back over newer states, a state with flag 2 (+0x28, no interrupt) blocks the replacement unless the owner is a valid actor with a brain and `CanStateInterrupt(0, brain +0xf4)` (vtable +0x138 in 2.123) agrees; a null first method means the fighter's current one (+0x220). The fighter agrees for `Fall(byte)` → `HitEvade(byte)` and `Block(class CAttackInfo,bool)` → `FromBlock`, `RightBlockCounter` or `LeftBlockCounter`, by method name. All of this sits behind "not replaying" (+0x78 clear): a replayed manager takes the disc's path, so the rule only matters to network lagged states, which the port does not have; it is kept as `can_state_interrupt` in `replay.cpp`.
217. **UI-manager natives and administrators (2.101)** [Core 2.123 `NextUIManagerCaller` 0x2004a510 (`DE_CAppManager::NextUIManager` 0x201a1570: `s_NextUIManager`'s target), `CurrentUIManagerCaller` 0x2004a530 (`GetUIManager`), `IsAdministratorCaller` 0x201fb310, `DE_CClient::AdministratorLevel` 0x201fcd30, `SetAdministratorLevel` 0x200142d0, `SetAsLocal` 0x201fbe60, `SetAsUserControlled` 0x201fc7a0; script `BRulesDisplay.AssureRulesDisplay`]: `NextUIManager()` is the page the app manager has queued, `CurrentUIManager()` the one up. The rules display closes the UI stack only when it is not the queued page itself. `IsAdministrator` follows a proxy client to its true client; a local client (+0x50) of a server session (its owner's, +0xcc, else its own +0x98) that a user controls (+0x68, the device, not −1) is an administrator, any other answers its level (+0x74, 0 until set). The port's sessions are local servers, so its local user clients are administrators; the "admin reset_game" chat command that asks stays unreachable without chat.
218. **Script frames' instances outlive the frame (2.101)** [Core 2.123 `DE_CClassCompiledMethod::CloseStack` 0x201423c0, `DE_CClassFactory::ObjUpdate` 0x201388b0, `g_ClassInstancesNeedingDestruction`]: closing a frame no longer destroys its class instances (CVector, CAttackInfo, CGenericEvent locals; vtable +0xd4); it queues them (class, frame value) and the class factory's update destroys them; the frame's behavior interfaces are still deleted at once. Ours never reuses an instance's bytes; a closed frame's instances are now queued as frames pop or a state restarts (callers dropped by KillCallers are not, as before), and forgotten (their class) at the start of the next world tick.
219. **Session values and the session's info (2.112)** [Core 2.123 `DE_CObject::ObjFindValueByName` 0x20040540 (vtable +0x108), `DE_CSession::ObjFindValueByName` 0x20202bd0, `DE_CObject::SetValue(const char*, int, int)` 0x200406d0 and the other accessors 0x200405b0..0x20041180, `DE_CSessionSettings::UIManagerComplete` 0x201ed930]: every value accessor finds its value through the new virtual. An object's looks for a child of that name; a session's asks its info (+0x38) first. A setter changes the value it finds, and only otherwise makes a new value on the object itself (when asked to create). The Choose Cast page's completion writes `StartingMatch` (its `g_StartingMatch`) into the settings' session info (+0x11c), which is how `Session().GetINTValue("StartingMatch")` sees it. The port gives each session an info object (`SessionState::description`) for this.
220. **`UnlockSinglePlayerMatch`, `RenderQuality`, `ReplaceWithThenDie`** [Core 2.123 `UnlockSinglePlayerMatchCaller` 0x201f8ae0, `UnlockCaller` 0x201f8aa0, `DE_CActor::RenderQuality` 0x20095260 / caller 0x200924b0, `DE_CRenderer::ObjInitContents` 0x20189b30, `SetMaximumQuality` 0x2018a5c0, `DE_CObject::ReplaceWithThenDie` 0x20043800]: `UnlockSinglePlayerMatch(n)` is the description's `Unlock` (vtable +0x13c) with id 0x10063 + n. `RenderQuality` is 0 unless the actor's scene has a current renderer with a camera (+0x58); else d = |actor − camera| − 100, raised to 1, and the result is min(120 / d, 1) × the renderer's +0x78. That field is its maximum quality: 1 from `ObjInitContents`, and no module or the launcher's imports call `SetMaximumQuality`. `ReplaceWithThenDie(r)` re-parents each child to r, points every reference to the object at r (`RefTargetReplace`) and queues the object for cleaning, also when r is null. The port re-points texture managers that draw a replaced image, by data object (so for every copy). The get-hit debris that uses both does not show in the port yet, because `BFighter.ResolveFighterMembers` finds neither the model's `RGBMain` property nor `GetHitBitsParent` on our fighters (it does not find `DestructionDebrisParent` with 2.090 data either): our fighters do not take their model's hookup.
221. **Creature interest from the contents; navigation's area search (2.123)** [Creature 2.123 `DE_CCreature::ObjProcessContents` 0x10214910, `DE_CNavigationLink::CPath::GetPathAffector` 0x1020ef70 (2.112 at the same address)]: after the actor's chunk, creature contents v2-v4 hold u32 +0x1dc (scene ticks per update), i32 +0x1e0 and f32 +0x1f4, the creature's own global interest (then v2 and v3 four BOOL flags into +0x1d0, v4 a u32 there and two squeezed values). It is 1 on the fighters and 0.05-0.5 on projectiles, crystals and the fighter jet; 2.123's data zeroed it only in Chronos and Mantis (fighter and projectiles) and on Force's projectiles (FINDINGS 232). It is the interest a creature starts with (ours started at 0). `GetPathAffector` tests the scene's sphere at the point with radius 0, then 5, then the radius squared while nothing at all is touched, giving up past 500, and returns the first touched affector that is on the path. When it touches only other affectors, 2.112 tested the same sphere again, forever; 2.123 grows it by 100 while it is under 500 (so 0, 100, …, 400 on a floor).
222. **The projectile's own methods** [OMF 2.112+ `DE_COMF2ProjectileFactory::Init` 0x1073e400 (disc 0x3503dd50), `NewExecutorlessCopyCaller` (disc 0x3503dc20); `?NewExecutorlessCopy@DE_COMF2Projectile`; script `BLaser.Bounce`]: on the disc the projectile factory registered `Owner`, `SetOwner`, `SetOrigional`, `NewExecutorlessCopy` and `IsTouchingArena` on `DE_CCreature`'s class node, so the disc's classes call them as `CCreature::…`; from 2.112 they hang on `COMF2Projectile`'s own node (`m_OMF2ProjectileClassNode`), and the recompiled classes (Jaguar's `PLaser`) call `COMF2Projectile::…` with `COMF2Projectile` parameter types. `NewExecutorlessCopy` copies the projectile (vtable +0xf4), carries its owner (+0x308) and creator (+0x314), and removes and destroys every ClassExecutor among the copy's properties; `Bounce` then adds `BounceLaserExecutor` itself.
223. **Patched data formats** [Core 2.123 `DE_CStandardImage::ObjProcessContents` 0x2019a170, `DE_CImage::ObjProcessContents` 0x20193530, `DE_CObject::ObjLoadExtendedContents` 0x20044820 (unchanged from the disc)]: the image chunk 2.095 grew by one byte is `DE_CStandardImage`'s v7 (v6 reads a u32 at +0x48, v7 adds two BOOLs at +0xa0 / +0xa8); the reader seeks to the chunk end, so the extra zero byte is never read, as ours. The "ObjExtCon00" EX key the diff showed on `TextureManager\Reflection` objects is the standard extended-contents key `ObjExtCon` with its NUL: a texture manager's per-face UVs (FINDINGS 123's layout), which a reflection layer reads but does not draw from. Both 2.090 and 2.123 Data pass `de-tests --all`.
224. **Replay bookkeeping: hold end and repeatable records** [Creature 2.123 `DE_CCreature::Tick` 0x10216990, `AssureSequenced`; FINDINGS 176, 212]: in `Tick` the held / re-taken-state test comes first, then physics and brain, then the state-changed hooks, and only then does a hold end (`AssureSequenced` once the tick count reaches +0x1e4, mode +0x300 cleared, +0x1e4 = the count) before the count goes up and +0x20c is set; ours ended the hold before physics. A record is stamped with `time(NULL)` and new GUIDs (agent and record); runs without a wall clock (screenshot, headless) now take the date from their own clock after a fixed day (2004-01-24) and the GUIDs from a fixed seed, so their recordings repeat too.
225. **The first page change in 2.123** [Core (2.123) `DE_CAppManager::ObjInitContents` 0x2019f690, `TransitionUIManager` 0x201a0910, `UpdateUI` 0x201a1720, `IsTransitionNeeded` 0x201a0820, `BeginUI` 0x201a1dc0, `SetNextUIManager` 0x201a12b0, `m_FirstTransition` 0x20356188 (initial 1); disc `BeginUI` 0x7019b9f0; 2004 launcher `DE_CGameLauncherWindow::ObjInitContents` / `ProcessEvent` (source/omfbg-exe)]: the app manager's transition state (+0x74) starts at 1 in every build, not 0, so the launcher's first page (the main menu, which `SetNextUIManager`'s first-UI path begins at once) is switched to by the "doors closed" step: no START is sent and nothing closes. From 2.095 that step activates the doors actor when it is inactive and sends it END (the opening). 2.123 adds `m_FirstTransition`: while it is set, state 0 sends no START, `UpdateUI` neither activates the doors nor calls `TransitionUIManager` unless the page finished (+0x78), and state 2 clears it and returns to state 0 at once, without waiting for the doors or deactivating them; `BeginUI` (the launcher's control event 0x3a) is an empty `ret`, where it had sent END (the 2.123 launcher no longer imports `SignalStartPageTransitionEnd`). So the main menu comes up behind one iris opening, sent by the first page change. Ours had started at state 0, which closed the doors over the empty screen first.
226. **2.101's UI stack close and `NextUIManager`** [Core (2.123) `CloseUIManagerStack` 0x201a2140, `NextUIManager` 0x201a1570, `NextUIManagerCaller` 0x2004a510, `CurrentUIManagerCaller` 0x2004a530 (`GetUIManager` 0x201a2520), `IsPersistantInstance`; script `BRulesDisplay.AssureRulesDisplay` (Session.DEObjects, 2.101 source diff)]: before each `SetNextUIManager` that makes a stacked page next, `CloseUIManagerStack` `DeleteThisLater`s the page queued next if it has not activated (+0xa0), is not its factory's persistent instance (+0x110) and is auto-destroy (+0xa4); the final `SetNextUIManager(0)` deletes nothing. `NextUIManagerCaller` returns `s_NextUIManager`, `CurrentUIManagerCaller` the current page. `AssureRulesDisplay` now calls `CloseUIManagerStack` only when `NextUIManager() != m_RulesDisplay`. With FINDINGS 208's mechanism: while the rules display is queued behind Choose Creature's closing doors it is neither unstacked nor deleted, and `Begin`'s `SetNextUIManager(this, this, 1)` returns at once (it is next), so Choose Creature is stacked by the first call only; once the rules display is current, the next call unstacks Choose Creature as before, so FINDINGS 208's reading of `IsDisplayed` still holds. Without the guard the delete removes the queued rules page every frame (Practice and the tournaments then never leave Choose Creature). Ours: `AppManager::close_stack` marks such a page deleted (the front end makes a new rules page for a deleted one), and `AppManager::next_ui_object` / `current_ui_object` give the natives the scripts' object of the queued / current page (`UIManager::script`: the rules display, or the script's UI manager behind a script-launched selector).
227. **Choose Cast and 2.112's Starting Match** [Core (2.123) `DE_CSession::SatisfyMasterRequirements` 0x20201790, `DE_CSessionMasterDescription::ObjProcessReferences` 0x20206fe0, `DE_CSessionSettings::Begin` 0x201ed660 / `Setup` 0x201ed6d0 / `UIManagerComplete` 0x201ed930 / `AddChosenSettingsToSession` 0x201ed7e0, `DE_CChooseCastRobot::DE_CChooseCastRobot` 0x201ee5a0 (Starting Match 0x201eeeaf..0x201ef0d4), `g_StartingMatch` 0x2036ab08; OMF `DE_COMF2Manager::ProcessEvent` (call at 0x1071616a)]: a session master description's references end with a BOOL-terminated list of required casts (+0xf0). After the session chooser, OMF's `ProcessEvent` makes the session, sets it as server and calls `SatisfyMasterRequirements`; when a required cast is met by none of the session's modifiers (+0x88, each one's cast list +0x48) it queues a `DE_CSessionSettings` page ("Choose Cast": a list of the unmet casts and of the casts under a fixed root, each a modifier list, and Available Arenas when the session asks for one), and the session begins when that page completes; with auto-select (+0x94) its `Begin` loads the last modifiers (registry `Session Options\Last Modifiers`) and completes at once if they satisfy the master. Every 2.123 single-player master requires the `Session Speed` cast (the multiplayer ones a score-limit cast too), so the original shows Choose Cast before Choose Pilot. 2.112 adds to its robot: `g_StartingMatch` = 0; if the master description has a String value `Matches` it is split at newlines; unless debugging, entry i >= 1 stays only if the description is not locked under id 0x10063 + i (vtable +0x144) and is blanked otherwise; the INT combo "Starting Match" on `g_StartingMatch` (its row scaled from 0.75) is added when more than one entry is unlocked; `UIManagerComplete` writes INT `StartingMatch` = `g_StartingMatch` to the session info. The port has no Choose Cast page (`AreMasterRequirementsSatisfied` answers true and the session begins at once), so the chooser has no page to sit on yet.
228. **Placement times and the earlier transparent layer** [Core (disc) `DE_CMesh::ObjProcessContents` 0x701275e0 (base chunk v4: name, flags +0x40, placement times +0x44), `PostProcessRenderMeshElement` 0x70128510, `DE_CRenderMaterialElement::SetPlacementTimes` 0x70030680 (keeps -127..128); Direct3D `DE_CGenericShader::AddStage`, `DE_CClipVolume::DrawShaders` (re/specs/render-order.md)]: a blended first pass of a mesh with positive placement times goes to clip-volume layer 6, drawn after the opaque layers and before layer 8 (particles, trailers, the other blended passes), and its static key gains 1000 x placement times per stage material. Particle renderers are meshes and read the same field. 2.101 sets it on Volcano's lava faces (25, 25, 35; the reflecting top face is additive and moves to layer 6), Iceberg's `Fog Renderer` (90) and meshes in FirePit (its floor visibly changes) and Iceberg. Ours reads it (`de::Mesh::placement_times`, `ParticleRendererDef::placement_times`) and draws layer 6 before layer 8 in the transparent pass.
229. **The observer camera, 2.096 to 2.123** [Creature (2.123) `DE_CObserveCommand::ObjInitContents` 0x10221fb0, `PositionWatchQuality` 0x10222510, `MajorMove` 0x10222900, `Update` 0x10223610, `DesiredViewAngle` 0x10224d50, `DE_CDigitalOrientationPropulsionExecutor::Tick` 0x102298e0; disc 0x15021e90, 0x150223f0, 0x15022780, 0x15023400, 0x15024a20, 0x15029500]: 2.096: the orbit height starts at 13 (was 17.5) and the move sub-command's required distance at 12 (was 20); a position's watch quality (lower is better) uses x3 (was x2) inside the ideal distance and adds |n_y^2 x 1.2| for the unit vector n from the watched box's centre; `MajorMove` sets the best sampled quality (+0x124) to 2 and takes its field of view from `DesiredViewAngle`, now atan(|box size| / d) x 57.29578 x (|n_y| x 0.2 + framing x 0.92) for the camera's distance d and direction n from the box centre (was atan(|size| / orbit radius) x framing x 1.2), which the per-frame easing also uses; each update the major timer loses (quality / best) x delta x 0.5 + delta x 0.5 (was delta), and a watched creature whose global interest is below the threshold sets the evaluate timer to -10000. 2.101: the propulsion executor no longer zeroes each local axis whose offset is within 0.9 x the required distance of the momentum's carry. 2.123: the major-move test is (major timer / 15000 x 0.5 + 1.1) x quality < best, after which a negative major timer is clamped to -1; `ObjInitContents` zeroes +0x124.
230. **Speaker frequency and ambient sounds** [Core (2.123) `DE_CSpeaker::SetActiveNoise` 0x202226a0, `DE_CScene::PlayNoiseAmbient` 0x20076b10]: from 2.112 the playing rate is (1 + CenterRand(noise variance)) x speaker frequency x noise frequency, clamped to at most 1.5. 2.123's `PlayNoiseAmbient` makes its 1e7 sphere at (0, 0, -10000) and parents it to the scene's current renderer's camera (renderer +0x160, camera +0x58; without one it is added to the scene there), so an ambient sound follows the listener from straight behind and stays centred. Ours places an ambient voice at (0, 0, -10000) in the listener's frame at every update.
231. **Smaller front-end changes, 2.096 to 2.123** [OMF (2.123) `DE_CChooseFighterUIManager::SetupControls` 0x10757490, `DE_CRulesDisplayRobot` ctor 0x10764380, `DE_CVCRControls::ObjUpdate` 0x10765e70, `DE_CChooseFighterModelPickerUIManagerRobot::UpdateModel` 0x10756780 / `ProcessIdleEvent` 0x10756760; OMF (2.097) `DE_CControlsPanelRobot::ProcessButtonClickEvent` 0x10760570, `DE_CControlCaptureRobot::CaptureControl` 0x107608c0; Core (2.123) `DE_CDialog3d::UserInformation` 0x200f8310, `DE_CButtonOverlay::MouseEnter` 0x2009fa30, `DE_CScene::Pause` 0x200768d0 / `Continue` 0x200768e0]: Choose Creature's Back takes Esc (`ClickOnKey(0x1b)`) from 2.096. 2.097 guards the controls page: the capture dialog may be gone after `DoModal`, and the selected action entry may be null. 2.123's rules display draws its three texts (match name 19, commentary 14, next match 16 VPU) in (0.8, 0.9, 1, 1), where they were (1, 1, 0.65), (1, 0.75, 0.8) and (0.8, 0.8, 1); PATCHES.md's "placement constants" there are these colours. The VCR panel's speed timing reads `gTime`, and the caret blink runs on the frame control's timers, which pulse on `gTime`: ours read the wall clock and now read `SystemTime` (`World::system_ms`, FINDINGS 212). The model picker's `UpdateModel` `Pause`s the picker scene (+0x104 = 1) and the robot's next idle event `Continue`s it, so the frame of a model change passes no time there. The notification's OK strip (FINDINGS 210) is `OffsetFromBottomWithLen(0, VPU(y))` with y the icon column's bottom offset, already converted from 60: VPU(VPU 60), 97 pixels at 768 high, where the screenshot put the box's centre about VPU 50 up. The hovered box's fill is `PURE_BLACK`, black at alpha 0 (Core initialiser 0x200f9150), so the grey seen inside the hovered OK is not the overlay's.
232. **A fighter file's own robot is outside the scene** [OMF `DE_CFighterDescription::NewResource` (2.123 0x10733930); Creature `DE_CCreature::InternalUpdateNeedInfo` (disc 0x15018400); both data sets' creature contents, read with a probe over every `Actor\Creature*` object]: the fighter in play is a copy of its file's robot; the robot itself is only loaded, never prepared into a scene (inference: the original's computer fighters engage although the robot's contents interest is 1), so its `NeedAffector` is not in the scene's collision and the need test sphere never finds it. Our port kept each fighter file's root objects as scene roots. While creatures started with interest 0 the robot was skipped by `AttackBestEnemy` (global interest > 0); with the contents' interest (FINDINGS 221: 1 on Jaguar, Gargoyle, Katana, Pyros, Warlord, Force and LoadScene's FighterTemplate in both 2.090 and 2.123 data) it became a candidate, the computer fighter was sent to attack the unprepared robot and stood still from then on. The 2.112 attack-type numbering (Light 4, the rest shifted up) is engine-internal: scripts reach `+0x4c` only through the named `AddAttackType*` / `IsAttackType*` natives (the disc scripts use Standard, Heat and TimeStop), so 2.090 data runs unchanged on it; only a get-hit packet recorded by a 2.090 engine would carry the old bits.
238. **A ControlCreator's panel, and why 2.123's help no longer wraps in ours** [Core (2.123) `DE_CControlCreator::CreateNewControl` 0x20224ee0, `ObjProcessContents` 0x20224c30, `DE_CDescription::NewLargeRepresentation` 0x201f98c0, `DE_CTextDisplayControl3d::ObjInitContents` 0x201045a0 / `New` 0x20104960 / `BuildWrappedText` 0x201049b0 / `ProcessEvent` 0x20104900, `DE_CAutoSizeVerticalRobot` 0x2009fb70 / `ProcessControlEvent` 0x2009faa0, `DE_CAutoSizeHorizontalRobot` 0x2009fe10 / 0x2009fd40; checked against og.jpg (the original's help at 1024 × 768)]: the contents' second int (+0x40) is the alignment, not a wrap flag. `CreateNewControl(0)` makes a page viewer and fills its page; nested creators (`CreateNewControl(1)`) make a plain control. The holder gets a stacking autosize robot along the layout's axis (+0x44: 0 and 2 downwards, 1 and 3 across) and, nested, a largest-child robot on the other axis; layouts 2 and 3 put each run of items of one class in a row (column) of their own, centred (+0x150 4 / 2) when the alignment is 2. A Value\String becomes `DE_CTextDisplayControl3d::New(text, 0, 0)`: the zero third argument sets +0x230, which makes `BuildWrappedText` delete the display's own page viewer, so the text always wraps to the display's width less `SizeHorScrollBarControl` (launcher VPU 24) and twice the font, and the display's LenY becomes the lines' height; the creator sets the font (+0x20c) to VPU(+0x48) and, when the alignment is 0, the display's ECenter (+0x234, default 2) to 0, which `BuildWrappedText` turns into left-aligned lines `OffsetFromLeft(1)`. An image is a white-filled control VPU(its size × the creator's float +0x4c) (0.8 on the match descriptions, which fits their 512-pixel pictures to the Window). A robot sets a length only when it comes to more than 0 (children without one are skipped when stacking, from 1 pixel). 2.123's help creators carry alignment 0 (2.090's 2), so its pages are left-aligned and wrapped, as og.jpg shows; ours read +0x40 as "wrap", so 2.123's lines ran past the page. Ours now builds that tree (`control_creator` in ui.cpp, `TextBlock` / `AutoSize` in controls) for a description's large representation, its page viewer's vertical bar scrolling by bar, track and wheel; the rules display's and medium representations' flowed text always wraps.
239. **The app manager's first five frames; the launch flash is the original's** [Core (2.123) `DE_CAppManager::UpdateUI` 0x201a1720 (`FramesToWait` 0x20356190, initial 5; disc 0x7034a0b8), `TransitionUIManager` 0x201a0910; script `BDoorMover` (OMFBGGUI, 2.123 source)]: `UpdateUI` returns at once while its static `FramesToWait` counts down from 5, so no page is made current for the first five frames and the screen shows only what is active (nothing of the front end in ours: black). Then the first transition step (state 1, FINDINGS 225) makes the main menu current, activates it, sets the main frame and backdrop by the page's flags, and only then activates the doors and sends END. `BDoorMover` parks its 16 iris leaves 100 units off at `Entry` ("so they don't appear when the game starts"), and on END (`SlideDoorsOut`) waits `m_OpenDelay` 55 ms of render ticks (skipping up to ten ticks longer than 150 ms as load lag) before placing the leaves on `IrisLeafPath` at 0.0001 and opening them. So the original too shows the main menu for the delay (three 20 ms frames in a screenshot run), then the iris nearly closed, then the opening; only the five empty frames were missing from ours, which now waits them out (`AppManager::frames_to_wait`).
240. **The drifting squares behind 2.123's menus** [OMFBGGUI (2.112 / 2.123) `MainMenuTunnelEffects\Spots\SpotsRenderer` (BillboardedQuad), `SpotGenerator`, `SpotmeshSpawner`, `SpotAcceleration`, on `V.Backdrop`; Direct3D `DE_CGenericShader::AddStage` 0x20033500 (disc)]: the squares are the backdrop's `SpotsRenderer` particles: additive (blend 0), colour (0.62, 0.86, 1) at alpha 0.09 (colour mode 2), billboards 5 × 5 times the particle size (mode 0), spawned from the spots mesh and drifting by `SpotAcceleration`. Through 2.112 the renderer textured them with `Blinklightimage` (a soft round light, in the `BevelSurfaceBlinkLights` folder); 2.123 deleted that folder and the renderer's reference with it (its contents are byte-identical), so the renderer has no image. `AddStage` makes the stage whatever the material holds, and a stage without a texture modulates by white in Direct3D's fixed pipeline (inference), so the original draws them as the plain translucent squares ours draws. Nothing to change.
241. **Buttons are push buttons; the grey inside the hovered OK** [Core (2.123) `DE_CButtonControl3d::ObjInitContents` 0x200c2270 (disc 0x700bd810), `New` 0x200c3770 (disc 0x700bea60), `CreateVisualControl` 0x200c28a0, `DE_CButtonRobot::ProcessControlEvent` 0x200ca280 / `SetUp` 0x200ca770, `DE_CControl3d::CreateVisualControl` 0x200e8d00, `SetFillColor` 0x200e5dd0, `SetFrameWidthOnly` 0x200e89a0, `DE_CGUIStandardInterface::CreateControl` 0x200f9be0; `DE_CDialog3d::UserInformation` 0x200f8310 (p123b/dec/userinfo.c); checked against notif.png]: `ObjInitContents` sets the type (+0x1f8) to 1 and `New` leaves it, so every `New` button, the notification's OK and the confirm dialog's Yes / No included, is a push button: `CreateButtonControl`'s 2-pixel up bevel with the launcher's ColorFace face (grey 0.5 at alpha 0.5, the 0.2 gradient). `re/specs/controls.md`'s "New makes type 0, a plain button" is wrong. That face is the grey inside the hovered OK; the overlay's frame child is made after the button, so its blue glow lies over it, and the box's own fill (black 0.2, clear when hovered) under it. Ours drew OK plain, so only the glow showed; OK and Yes / No are now push buttons and the glow is painted after them. The screens' other `New` buttons (Options' Apply, Controls' Assign / Remove / Set Defaults, the Replays' Delete / Rename, the VCR's Snapshot, Choose Creature's View) are push buttons too and are still drawn plain.
